#2595. Knicks 121, Cavs 102: Scenes from a second-half bench bash

The Knicks' hopes of repeating as NBA Cup champions hinged on beating Cleveland (9-10) tonight and Miami on Friday, while Philadelphia had to beat Miami (which they did) and Indiana has to topple Philadelphia. Even then, advancement would come down to point differential, meaning New York (12-6) would likely need to win both games by double digits.

Consider the mission half completed. As you could deduce from the blazing blue court at Madison Square Garden, tonight's tilt was a Cup game. Three Knicks were sidelined by injury: OG Anunoby, Miles McBride, and Jordan Clarkson. Anunoby's absence was felt as the Cavaliers scored 61 first-half points (and a lopsided free-throw disparity stung, too). The adjustments Mike Brown made in the locker room paid dividends on the court. His squad applied stingier defense, got their turnovers under control, won the fourth quarter 39-17, and flipped the game to win, 121-102.

Of the six Knicks with double-digit points tonight, Jose Alvarado's barrage of fourth-quarter triples and his season-high / team-high 21 were arguably the most impactful. On a short-handed night, and with multiple starters playing through injury, Jose was once again prepared to step into the spotlight and contribute meaningful minutes. What a professional! What depth!

Three consecutive turnovers didn't help (two by Karl-Anthony Towns, one by Josh Hart) as the Knicks fell behind early in the first quarter. Midway through the period, Jalen Brunson scored seven in under two minutes to regain the lead. In this streaky contest, though, it made sense that he missed his next five attempts. Towns (12 PTS, 10 RBS, 7 AST, +21) was a game-time decision due to knee soreness, and his scoring showed the limp, producing a single point in the frame. Filling in for Anunoby in the first five, Landry Shamet made his sixth start of the season. He finished with a tidy 11 points on three made three-pointers.

The quarter was competitive until the final three minutes. After Pacôme Dadiet (yes, him!) drilled a triple to pull the Knicks within one, Cleveland rattled off a 10-0 run, powered by five consecutive points from Meleek Thomas, two Thomas Bryant free throws, and a Donovan Mitchell and-one. The latter easily had the biggest impact on the quarter, tallying 14 points, three dimes, and a steal. As for New York's bench support, Dadiet (7 PTS, 14 MIN) provided positive defensive efforts with a block and a steal, but neither Tyler Kolek nor Andre Drummond moved the needle. New York managed just six points over the final five-ish minutes, and Cleveland closed the quarter ahead, 34-23.

To start the second, the good guys outscored the villains by ten, chiseling an 11-point deficit to one. Credit Towns and his four points, two dimes, and two blocks for the comeback. Mohamed Diawara lent a triple, too, and Mikal Bridges found his rhythm, blasting out seven fast points. Naturally, Cleveland responded with a 9-2 stretch when James Harden (28 PTS, 1-8 3PT) finally got on the board. Dadiet drizzled water on our good feelings by missing three straight shots, and Brunson still couldn't regain his touch, going scoreless from the field in the period.

Playing from 55-44 down with four minutes left in the half, the hosts posted 11 unanswered points, eight of which belonged to Bridges. He'd finish the half with 17 points. This, my friends, is what's known as a circus shot:

Despite dealing with a sore shoulder, Hart picked Harden's pocket twice, converting one steal into a layup. Unfortunately, after Bridges tied the game at 55, Donovan Mitchell (30 PTS, 1-7 3PT) and Harden combined for six unanswered points, aided by a Bridges turnover. The home team won the quarter 33-27 but entered intermission trailing 61-56.

Through the half, Harden and Mitchell combined for 34 points in the half, while their bench contributed 13. New York had shot better from the field (54% to 49%) and significantly better from deep (50% to 31%), they were on the wrong end of the score due to a glaring free-throw disparity. New York had attempted just eight freebies, while Cleveland shot more than twice that. The Cavs outscored won the paint, 28-16, and the boards. Another glaring stat: Cleveland converted six Knicks miscues into 13 points, while New York squeezed just two points out of five giveaways.

Cleveland opened the second half on an 11-5 run to restore its double-digit lead. Down 74-65 midway through the period, the Knicks got a boost from an unexpected source: Tyler Nickel. The rookie sank a triple, followed by another from Landry Shamet, to cut the deficit to three. Nickel contributed five points but also committed four fouls in under six minutes—that will get straightened out with experience. Harden extended Cleveland's advantage to 85-77 with under three minutes remaining. But then another unexpected player pitched in: James Wiseman provided some valuable minutes off the bench, finishing the game with six points and seven boards in six minutes. Shamet helped the Knicks win the quarter 26-24 and enter the fourth trailing 85-82.

The Knicks opened the fourth quarter on a 12-0 run, turning a three-point deficit into a nine-point advantage in less than three minutes. Jose Alvarado (a team-high 21 points, 8-10 FG, 19 MIN) was the catalyst, drilling consecutive triples and picking Donovan Mitchell's pocket to set up a Dadiet layup. Hart took a turn orchestrating the offense, collecting four assists during the opening surge. The Ohio Players missed their first five shots, four of them by Mitchell, and committed two turnovers as the Knicks piled on the points.

Wiseman (!) helped goose New York's lead to 13 with four points and three offensive rebounds in a brief fourth-quarter stint. When Mitchell and Harden scored five unanswered points to trim the deficit to eight, Alvarado responded with a jumper, and Jalen Brunson buried a triple, his only basket of the quarter. Alvarado was the unquestionable star of the fourth, scoring 16 of his 21 points, including four three-pointers. Hart was nearly as impactful, contributing eight points, six assists, three rebounds, a steal, and a block in the frame. The Knicks closed on a 7-0 run, their defense holding Cleveland scoreless over the final two minutes and change.

Welcome back to associate head coach Chris Jent, who returned to the sidelines after a month away.

The Knicks hang around town to face the Heat on Friday in the last of their first-stage Cup games. Mo is folding and pressing your recap. Happy Knicksgiving, turkey fans.

#2591. Packers Snap Counts: Micah Parsons played less than 40 percent of snaps

Micah Parsons made his 2026 debut tonight, and he was certainly on a pitch count. Let's take a look at every player who saw action (or didn't) in the Green Bay Packers' 26-13 loss to the Los Angeles Rams tonight.

As a reminder, the scratches today were running back Josh Jacobs (finger), linebacker Ziare Franklin (shin), offensive lineman Donovan Jennings, offensive lineman Jager Burton and offensive lineman Mekhi Becton (all three were healthy scratches to get down to a 48-man game-day roster).

The Packers' top two backs in this game were Kaleb Johnson and Chris Brooks, after there was a three-way split last week. Johnson was given a more all-around back role, as he was targeted twice and ran the ball eight times. Last week, Johnson was the runner, Brooks was the blocking back and MarShawn Lloyd was the route-runner.

When Josh Jacobs returns to the lineup, assume that Lloyd is going to get knocked down to being a healthy scratch. The Packers haven't ever had more than three backs up on the 48-man game-day roster, even when they held five players at the position on the 53-man active roster.

Receiver Christian Watson dropped out of the game with a concussion, which is why Savion Williams jumped him in the pecking order this week. Williams actually started the game as the slot receiver, a role that Skyy Moore was playing until Moore's ankle got injured earlier in the year. In his two weeks back from injury, Moore has only played one offensive snap.

Tucker Kraft played every single snap of the game, and the Packers only played 1.3 tight ends per play, a big difference from their game plan against the Minnesota Vikings last week. Last week, the team heavily leaned into multiple tight end looks, while this week featured a lot more three-receiver sets, even when Christian Watson went down.

The starting offensive line in this game was Jordan Morgan, Laken Tomlinson, Sean Rhyan, Kevin Zeitler and Anthony Belton (left to right), a lineup that had never played together before. Zeitler dropped out of action in the middle of the game, though, which pushed Aaron Banks, who had been benched in recent weeks, into a guard spot.

Earlier in the season, rookie third-round pick Chris McClellan was a reserve defensive lineman, but because of injuries, the nose tackle played the most snaps of anyone in the unit against the Rams. Devonte Wyatt, who appeared to be on a pitch count in the first half (he hasn't looked right coming off his ankle injury this year), also dropped out of the game due to injury. It will be interesting to see if the team tries to get Warren Brinson, who is currently on the injured reserve, up to the active roster soon.

Micah Parsons' return to action featured him playing 39 percent of the defensive snaps in this game, about half of his playing time rate in 2025. For the most part, the snaps for Parsons, Barryn Sorrell (who got injured in this game, Brenton Cox Jr. and Dani Dennis-Sutton split snaps about evenly behind Lukas Van Ness.

With Zaire Franklin still out, Isaiah McDuffie played the Mike spot and Ty'Ron Hopper was the Will. When Franklin comes back, which should be soon, he should be playing the Mike. When Franklin was last healthy, McDuffie and Hopper split the Will position.

The only notable nugget here is that Kamal Hadden played one snap in the slot when Javon Bullard was getting looked at for an injury. Otherwise, Brandon Cisse played right cornerback and Kamal Hadden played left cornerback, as usual.

Because the Rams played such heavy personnel on offense, it forced the Packers to play their 3-4 base defense often. The result of that was Javon Bullard, the team's slot defender, only playing 31 percent of the defensive snaps today. "Nickel is the new base" is a fun catchphrase for NFL analysis right now, but it all depends on what the opposing offense puts on the field. Unfortunately, the Packers didn't get many three-receiver looks today, in part because of how the game got out of hand for them in the second half. That left Bullard on the bench for more than two-thirds of the plays tonight.

Day'Ron Sharpe, who scored 11 points and grabbed 14 rebounds, slams home a dunk during the Nets' win over the Bucks in Milwaukee. Jeff Hanisch-Imagn Images

#2586. Where to watch Bills vs. Chiefs today: Channel, live stream, time, TV schedule for NFL Thanksgiving game

Where to watch Bills vs. Chiefs today: Channel, live stream, time, TV schedule for NFL Thanksgiving game originally appeared on The Sporting News. Add The Sporting News as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

#2580. Rapid Recap: Nets 104, Bucks 97

The Milwaukee Bucks follow a string of solid games with one of their worst in a long time, losing at home to the Brooklyn Nets, 104-97. Tyler Herro led the Bucks with 28 points and five assists, while Kel'el Ware led the Bucks bench with 13 points and 14 rebounds. Julius Randle led the Nets with 28 points, and Noah Clowney led their bench with 17 points.

The game started with the Bucks on the attack, forcing three personal fouls in the first 90 seconds and exploiting the mismatches created when four forwards and one guard are the opposing starting five. Myles Turner wasted no time pulling out his three-ball from Toronto as the Bucks quickly built a 9-2 lead. But of course, the Nets started finding their shot as the Bucks let their feet off the gas, going on a 16-2 run as Milwaukee's offence dried up like the Sahara Desert. The Bucks didn't score another field goal until the 4:08 mark, when Jaime Jaquez Jr. finally buried a three, which set off an 8-2 run that included a sweet baseline dunk by Kel'el Ware. Despite being in the bonus for more than eight minutes, the Bucks couldn't take advantage, shooting only six free throws for the quarter and ending the period down 28-24.

The second quarter started where the first quarter ended: with Ryan Rollins throwing up ill-advised shots—he was bad in this one. Meanwhile, the Nets steadily built a 41-28 lead through the first five minutes. Thankfully, the young Bucks off the bench played their typical brand of high-energy defense and held Brooklyn to six points over the next five minutes, but unfortunately, the home side couldn't buy a bucket themselves. Milwaukee did at least outscore Brooklyn 11-4 in the final few minutes to cut the deficit to four, with Ware giving fans a reason to get hyped with a monster dunk before Jaquez scored a quick lay-up to end the half on a high note. Milwaukee headed into the locker room down 47-51.

Coming out of halftime, the Bucks kept up the pace and quickly regained the lead, largely thanks to 11 quick points from Herro, and it finally felt like the home team had found a flow. Unfortunately, that flow ended as Rollins decided to dominate the ball, force shots, and make head-scratching decisions in general. Eventually, the Nets crept back ahead after another scoring drought by the Bucks late in the third quarter. If not for Ware imposing his will in the paint, the Nets may have run away with it. Instead, the big man finished the third quarter with 10 points and 10 rebounds, scoring the final four to keep the deficit at six, 77-71.

The Nets extended their lead to 10 pretty quickly to open the final frame, as the Bucks struggled to get paint touches, something that seemed like it would come easier after getting in the paint so effectively against Philadelphia and Toronto. Milwaukee made a few pushes, but each one ended in an unforced error; they just couldn't get out of their own way. The game ended ugly as the Bucks turned it over nine times in the fourth quarter alone to give this one away. One of the more disappointing efforts of the last few years, frankly, and that's saying something.

26: the number of free throw attempts the Nets had, which allowed them to hang in the game despite their horrid shooting.

Ryan Rollins needs to be glued to the bench for a game (or five).

#2574. Rams shut down Packers in second half for a 26-13 win in NFL's first Thanksgiving Eve game

The Rams and Packers played to a tie in the first 30 minutes of Wednesday night’s game in Los Angeles, but the second half was all Rams.

Blake Corum ran for 151 yards and a score in final 30 minutes and the Rams defense shut out the Packers to pave the way to a 26-13 win. The victory is the fifth in a row for the Rams and they are now 7-4 with a Thursday night home game against the Chiefs on deck in Week 13.

The win moves the Rams ahead of the Packers in the race for NFC playoff spots and Thursday’s game between the Lions and Bears will have further impact on the chances for all four teams to advance to the postseason.

Corum’s touchdown came at the end of the Rams’ first possession of the second half and he finished the game with a career-high 32 carries for 184 yards.

That scoring drive ate up more than eight minutes. The Packers had a quick three-and-out, but briefly showed some life when linebacker Ty’Ron Hopper picked off Matthew Stafford to set the offense up in Los Angeles territory. Jordan Love turned the ball right back over when he hung a deep pass that safety Jaylen McCullough snagged out of the air. The Rams would then ride Corum and Kyren Williams on a pair of drives that covered more than 11 minutes.

The Packers’ last chance came with 6:13 left and they moved the ball to midfield before Love threw his second interception of the game. Cornerback Emmanuel Forbes got that one and the Packers were out of timeouts well before the two-minute warning.

Sticking with the ground game helped to limit Packers defensive end Micah Parsons’s impact in his return from last season’s torn ACL. He had a sack late in the first half, but Stafford only threw eight times after the break and the Packers’ run defense could not get their offense the ball often enough to get back into the game.

Love was 17-of-29 for 198 yards and a touchdown, but clearly missed wide receiver Christian Watson after he left the game with a concussion. They only picked up two first downs after halftime and they’ll hope that an extended break before their Week 13 trip to New Orleans is enough time for him to return to action.

The Rams have several injuries of their own to monitor as guard Bill Murray joined tight ends Davis Allen and Tyler Higbee in making early exits from the game. They’d like to have all those players back next week, but they showed on Wednesday night that Corum can sometimes be enough all by himself.

#2572. Nets snap a five game losing streak, beat the Bucks 104-97

The Brooklyn Nets and Milwaukee Bucks came into tonight's game like two school kids trotting onto the playground, one pointing and chirping at the other "you're the worst defense," while the other bickered back "no, you're the worst defense."

Each youngster had some merit to their argument. Brooklyn had just given up 130+ in three of their last four games, and ranked dead last in defensive rating ahead of the contest. Milwaukee ranked behind them by a few fractions of a point.

Brooklyn would eventually win the squabble, and the game, but it'd take them some time to get there, not that anyone's complaining here.

Brooklyn came out the gates without a whole lot of ball pressure. In fairness, they had Danny Wolf matched up initially against Tyler Herro, which leaves you very little spatial insurance to work with. However, the Nets also looked out of sync rotating around the Milwaukee offense, which didn't help either. As Brooklyn shot 1-6 from the field and the Bucks pranced out to a 9-2 lead, it just looked like more of the same in the early going.

As I mentioned, we'd eventually find out otherwise. And while real turnaround would soon come on the defensive end, things had to start for Brooklyn at the other.

After Milwaukee's opening jolt, the Nets went on a 13-0 run in the heart of the first to go up 22-12. Michael Porter Jr., who spoke to Brain Lewis this week about how he's struggled to "find his rhythm," was in his grove amid the stretch, putting in eight first period points.

Getting five from Day'Ron Sharpe and Julius Randle each, as well as eight points off the bench in the period, the Nets led after one by a 28-24 margin.

Despite being less of a scorer this time around, Mikel Brown Jr. looked comfortable out there early on as well, starting for a second straight game. He shot 0-5 from the field in the first half but clocked in as a +4 with two assists with zero turnovers.

Also like last game, Grant Nelson joined him on the floor for a few minutes in the second. Nelson only finished with one point, but grabbed six rebounds, contributing to a strong collective effort from the team on the glass, where the Nets won 52-44. Porter Jr. also grabbed 11 rebounds, his most in a game all season if you discount the overtime affair vs Boston.

Brooklyn would need every one of those boards especially in the first half, where neither team shot it at a particularly high clip, as the Nets posted .400/.348 splits, while the Bucks had .432/.273 marks.

At the break, Brooklyn led 51-47, largely thanks to Randle, who had 16 points on 7-10 shooting from the field and 2-4 from deep. MPJ went quiet in the second, still rocking with eight points by then. Day'Ron Sharpe put us on early triple-double watch with five points, 10 rebounds, and four assists at the break.

Herro, however, had all the stats to begin the third. He poured in nine points during the period's first four minutes to put his team back in the lead for the first time since the 6:57 mark of the first. The Nets, meanwhile, opened the period 3-12 from the field and 0-5 from deep. Two of the makes came from Brown Jr, so at least there's that.

Sarcasm aside, as one young guard for Brooklyn started to turn it on, another followed. After Milwaukee looked ready to pull away, Egor Dëmin checked in, scoring five points himself and assisting on two baskets in just a minute and a half. His effort helped ignited a 13-2 run that put the lead back in Brooklyn's hands.

At the same time, Randle continued to mine inside for points. He'd eventually go for 28 in the game on 10-16 shooting. That not only gave Brooklyn consistent offense, but slowed things down and allowed them to set up easily at the other end. Even with Herro's early offensive excellence, the Bucks went 7-23 from the field in the quarter, and the Nets led going into the fourth by a 77-71 score.

"I think everybody was able to just put their minds together and not just focus on team defense, but their own island, like how we say 'guard your yard,'" Dëmin said. "I think that's where it came from. Everything else was easier because everybody was so locked in and super focused on how to guard the man in front of you rather than relying on someone else around."

And believe it or not, in that period, the defense continued to improve. On Milwaukee's second possession of the frame, Porter Jr. poked the a ball away in the backcourt, which Nelson dove across mid court to save. Still, the Bucks got it up and forward, but then Keon Ellis came charging over, intercepting the rock and then dishing it ahead Porter Jr. for an and-one finish. That was just one of the Nets seven steals in the fourth.

"Go out there and play with physicality," Sharpe said. "Go out there and play with ball pressure. Execute with staples and have each others back. I felt like we did a great job at that tonight."

Sharpe never got the triple double, but did finish 11 points, five assists, and 14 rebounds while shooting 5-10 from the field and hitting a three.

The Nets got up by 11 early in the fourth. After a few turnovers early in the frame, the Bucks cut it back down to eight, and we looked in store for a tight finish.

But in a game where Brooklyn's supposed fatal flaw had carried them, it was only right that Noah Clowney, who's struggled with his shot all year, iced things out. In the fourth, he added nine points on 4-6 shooting, scoring from all over the court to keep the lead afloat. He finished with a season-high 17 points, also grabbing five rebounds while shooting 7-13 from the field.

"This is the Noah Clowney that we need," Fernández said. "It gives us a starter off the bench and it's very, very good for this group, because it brings a player on both ends."

Dëmin, who's also had a difficult start to the year, leveraged his size to play some inspiring defense down the stretch as well, finishing as a +5 in the quarter. For the game, he had 15 points, four assists, three rebounds, a steal, and a block while shooting 5-11 from the field.

"Yeah, he went back to doing the simple things, and it started with his physicality on defense," Fernández said. "They felt him the whole game. His length. He can rebound, he can be in the gaps…Proud of him for sure."

Sure, the Nets still turned it over on back-t0-back possession with the Bucks only down 11 and under a minute to go. Dëmin missed also missed an open dunk. They're still a young, flawed team. They didn't solve all their issues tonight, but they did win the night, and snapped their five game losing streak.

Final: Brooklyn Nets 104, Milwaukee Bucks 97

Mo Wagner subbed out at the end of the first period and did not return for the rest of the game. At half, the Nets ruled him out with left knee soreness.

"I don't know. I was about to sub him in, and I saw him limping, and I was like I got to save him from himself," Fernández said. "He was there to do whatever it took for his team and his teammates, and at the end I decided to not bring him back in. Obviously, I don't know what it is, but the good thing, the great thing is he was cheering for everybody. He was cheering fro Grant [Nelson] the whole time, and that means a lot for the group, and it speaks to the type of person he is."

We'll provide updates on him once they're made available.

Day'Ron Sharpe had a season-high 14 rebounds tonight and also recorded his 5th double-double of the season with 11 points. It matches his double-double total from last season, tied as the 2nd most in his career (6, 2023-24). This was his 7th career game with 10+ PTS, 10+ REB and 5+ AST.

Noah Clowney had a season-high 17 points off the bench tonight against the Bucks. He has tied his season high with three 3-pointers made.

Michael Porter Jr. logged his third double-double of the season with 12 points and 11 rebounds. It is his 15th time recording at least 10 rebounds as a Net.

Brooklyn gets right back in action after the holiday with a road matchup against the Toronto Raptors on Friday. The North has lost seven of their last eight, including a contest to the Bucks on Monday. They're certainly looking at this one as a "get right" game. The game tips off at 7:30 p.m. EST.

#2568. T.J. Lang blasts "despicable" Aaron Rodgers comments about Mike McCarthy

T.J. Lang blocked for Aaron Rodgers for eight seasons in Green Bay. Currently, Lang would be more inclined to knock Rodgers on his ass.

Lang sharply criticized Rodgers for his decision to go public with issues that should have stayed in the Pittsburgh locker room.

“I think it’s despicable,” Lang said on 97.1 The Ticket in Detroit, via Sean Keeley of Awful Announcing. “I think that Aaron Rodgers just became everything that he has bitched about and moaned about over the last 10 years with former coaches, former teammates, airing grievances out, giving opinions out in the media.

“He has blacklisted, I don’t know how many, former teammates from just simple comments telling the truth because he just — he was sick of it. Honestly, I don’t even know if I want to watch the whole thing. It’s just unnecessary. It’s a victim mentality. It’s a ‘poor me, woe is me. Oh, I thought this, and it wasn’t that.’ And I’m sick, man.

“I’ve been in a group chat with a whole lot of former teammates that feel the exact same way that I do.”

Most football players would feel that way. There’s a code in the locker room. Rodgers violated it.

“Mike McCarthy was my head coach for 8 years, and I love Mike McCarthy,” Lang said. “And I talked to Mike McCarthy on Monday night, and still have a great relationship with him.

“I haven’t talked to Aaron Rodgers in probably 5 years. I can’t remember the last time he returned a text. I don’t have a relationship with him. And I think it’s just absolutely despicable what he just did to Big Mike.”

Rodgers isn’t a whistleblower. He’s a troublemaker. He didn’t get his way, so he emptied a can of gasoline and tossed a match over his shoulder on the way out the door.

How does any of it help his now-former teammates? Rodgers’s decision to air out dirty laundry has created a massive distraction that will do nothing to help the team win.

Accurate or not, the task at hand becomes harder now that everyone is buzzing about Rodgers’s comments.

That said, no one should be surprised. It’s part and parcel of doing business with Rodgers. When it ends — and end it will — it will end poorly. Like it did in Green Bay. Like it did in New York. Like it did in Pittsburgh.

Like it will end for his next team, if there is one. And if there is, the message is clear.
